THE BUTTERFLY EFFECT

The machine stops screaming before you do.

You sit strapped in, sweating, your hands shaking on the armrests. It was a systems test. Nothing was
supposed to go anywhere. The panels are dark and the controls are dead.

You open the hatch expecting the concrete floor of the test bay. You get dirt. A mule cart rattles
past close enough to touch, and the driver shouts at you in Latin. You understand him.

Thank God I took that in college.

Ancient Rome. Not ruins. Alive.

The machine is dead weight. Its contacts and couplings are gold, and gold you can carry: you pry out
60 aurei worth of it. Every piece will have to go back before the machine could ever run again.
Rome pays in silver denarii. Gold has to be changed at the money changers (exchange <n> aurei).

Two things on this street could use your money, and you can afford only one:
  choose fountain  — The Subura's fountain runs brown. Clear the lead pipe and reline the basin.
  choose workshop  — Pay for a second forge and paid apprentices at the smith's by the Porta Trigemina, in exchange for a share of the business.
Your ears are still ringing. Before anything else you could walk and get your bearings: visit market, changers, forges, curia or subura (no Attention).
Type 'help' for commands.

== Month 1 · Ianuarius, AD 155 ==  0 denarii (+0/month) · 60 aurei (1 = 25 den.)   Index 100

  ► Waiting: choose fountain or choose workshop (before the end of turn 8).

> choose workshop
You chose the workshop.  [Attention left this month: 2/4]

> why medicine
Medicine is at 50. You have no voice in it, so it follows Rome's real history (+0 last year). Only your projects and their consequences move it.
Your influence over it: 0%, so your priorities and policy take 0% effect. Who holds it:
  The healers of the Tiber Island sanctuary: strength 50, 62.5% of Medicine
  The Physicians' Circle of the Subura: strength 30, 37.5% of Medicine
People expect 50: what was normal in Rome in AD 155.
Debt 0 (Stable). Debt grows 5% a year until paid down, which costs 75 denarii per point. Each point of it makes the plague hit harder (hazard +0.3 a point).
Index sub-score 100 (100 = as in real history).

Governance is at 55. You have no voice in it, so it follows Rome's real history (+0 last year). Only your projects and their consequences move it.

> why gold
You have 0 denarii and 20 aurei (an aureus now costs 25 denarii at the changers, before their fee).
Income 0 denarii a year: none. You earn by working (work odd / craft / consult); owned property and well-run institutions add income.
Work is taxed at 10%. Domains: Medicine runs without you, Governance runs without you, Economy runs without you.
Settled each month: 0 denarii per month.
Recent changes:
  AD 155-01  You pay in the machine's gold: 40 aurei. [+40]
  AD 155-01  Work begins: Buy into the smith's workshop (1,000 denarii, 4 months). [-40]
      because: You can afford only one: you choose the smith's workshop over the district fountain. (AD 155-01)

> why plague
No sign of pestilence yet. History says one reaches Rome from the East in AD 166, with its first rumors in Iulius, AD 165.
If it broke out now with no response, about 10% of Rome would die (history: 10%):
  Hazard 60 (history 60) = base 50 + Medicine debt 0 × 0.3 + foul water 10.
  Resilience 38.8% (history 38.8%) from Medicine, Governance, preparations and institutions you steer.

> why circle
The Physicians' Circle of the Subura, led by Demetria of Pergamon: strength 30, 37.5% of Medicine.
You hold 0%: nothing yet. To join it asks for a finished Medicine project (the fountain, a physician, or quarantine rules) or your promise to Demetria (you don't yet); joining costs 430 denarii (entry fee 250 denarii + the first 1%), then dues of 50 denarii a year plus 2 denarii per percent you hold.

> why faction
The Caecilian faction, led by Senator Lucius Caecilius Varro: strength 45, 47.4% of Governance.
You hold 0%: nothing yet. To join it asks for consulting for wealthy households at least 2 times, or the senator's patronage project; and less than 10% of the Junians (you don't yet); joining costs 1,020 denarii (entry fee 750 denarii + the first 1%), then dues of 125 denarii a year plus 2 denarii per percent you hold.

> why promise
No one has asked anything of you yet.

> why index
Index 100: the geometric mean of the three sub-scores, so balance counts.
  Medicine: 50 vs 50 in real history = 100
  Governance: 55 vs 55 in real history = 100
  Economy: 55 vs 55 in real history = 100

> why attention
You have 2 of 4 Attention left this month. Attention never grows; it is spent on projects, overseeing an institution, or your one personal action.

The fuller says the merchant should sacrifice to the river and stop complaining.

> why plague
No sign of pestilence yet. History says one reaches Rome from the East in AD 166, with its first rumors in Iulius, AD 165.
If it broke out now with no response, about 10.2% of Rome would die (history: 10%):
  Hazard 60.6 (history 60) = base 50 + Medicine debt 2.1 × 0.3 + foul water 10.
  Resilience 38.3% (history 38.8%) from Medicine, Governance, preparations and institutions you steer.

> why circle
The Physicians' Circle of the Subura, led by Demetria of Pergamon: strength 30, 37.5% of Medicine.
You hold 0%: nothing yet. To join it asks for a finished Medicine project (the fountain, a physician, or quarantine rules) or your promise to Demetria (you don't yet); joining costs 455 denarii (entry fee 265 denarii + the first 1%), then dues of 50 denarii a year plus 2 denarii per percent you hold.

> why promise
No one has asked anything of you yet.

> why medicine
Medicine is at 49. You have no voice in it, so it follows Rome's real history (+0 last year). Only your projects and their consequences move it.
Your influence over it: 0%, so your priorities and policy take 0% effect. Who holds it:
  The healers of the Tiber Island sanctuary: strength 50, 62.5% of Medicine
  The Physicians' Circle of the Subura: strength 30, 37.5% of Medicine
People expect 50: what was normal in Rome in AD 159.
The shortfall of 1 adds that much debt each year.
Debt 2.1 (Stable). Debt grows 5% a year until paid down, which costs 75 denarii per point. Each point of it makes the plague hit harder (hazard +0.3 a point).
Index sub-score 98 (100 = as in real history).
Recent changes:
  AD 157-01  Fever in the district: children who drink from the broken fountain fall sick. You remember choosing the workshop over the fountain. [-1]
      because: You can afford only one: you choose the smith's workshop over the district fountain. (AD 155-01)
  AD 158-01  Medicine debt grows to 1: level 49 is below expectation 50. [+1]
      because: Fever in the district: children who drink from the broken fountain fall sick. You remember choosing the workshop over the fountain. (AD 157-01)
  AD 159-01  Medicine debt grows to 2.1: level 49 is below expectation 50. [+1.1]
      because: Fever in the district: children who drink from the broken fountain fall sick. You remember choosing the workshop over the fountain. (AD 157-01)

  Attention: 4 free / 4 total

> why plague
The pestilence has passed.
It struck in AD 166: severity 35.5, about 106.4 thousand dead; response: none.
Recent changes:
  AD 166-07  Warning 3 of 3 — Fever at Ostia and in the Subura: physicians report fever, rash and black stools among the poor.
  AD 166-10  Outbreak — the pestilence breaks out across Rome as the city celebrates the army's triumph.
      because: Warning 3 of 3 — Fever at Ostia and in the Subura: physicians report fever, rash and black stools among the poor. (AD 166-07)
  AD 166-10  The pestilence is severe: about 106 thousand dead, 11% of Rome. [-106.4]
      because: Fever in the district: children who drink from the broken fountain fall sick. You remember choosing the workshop over the fountain. (AD 157-01)
      because: Medicine debt grows to 11: level 49 is below expectation 50. (AD 166-01)
      because: Outbreak — the pestilence breaks out across Rome as the city celebrates the army's triumph. (AD 166-10)
      because: No organized response: each household fends for itself. (AD 166-10)
  AD 166-10  The sickness burns itself out. Nothing is fixed; there are simply fewer people left, and the survivors expect less.
      because: The pestilence is severe: about 106 thousand dead, 11% of Rome. (AD 166-10)

> why gold
You have 27,221 denarii and 20 aurei (an aureus now costs 33.7 denarii at the changers, before their fee).
Income 133 denarii a year: property you own 133 denarii.
Work is taxed at 10%. Domains: Medicine runs without you, Governance runs without you, Economy runs without you.
Settled each month: 11 denarii per month.
Recent changes:
  AD 174-09  You spend the month mending tools and running errands for pay. [+5.2]
  AD 174-10  You spend the month mending tools and running errands for pay. [+5.2]
  AD 174-11  You spend the month mending tools and running errands for pay. [+5.2]
  AD 174-12  You spend the month mending tools and running errands for pay. [+5.2]

== Month 243 · Martius, AD 175 ==  25,746 denarii (+11/month) · 34 aurei (1 = 33.7 den.)   Index 101.3
  Attention: 4 free / 4 total
  Medicine    43.1  expect 44.4  (no voice)  debt  15.2 Stable
  Governance  51.4  expect 51.5  (no voice)  debt   1.4 Stable
  Economy     53.3  expect 53.3  (no voice)  debt     0 Stable
  Prices: +34.7% since AD 155 (1.5% a year)
  Pestilence: The pestilence has passed.
  Workshop orders: take ironwork (539 denarii, 3 Att.); take tools (219 denarii, 2 Att.)   (workshop)
  ► Cassianus: commission look cellarpump (unpaid)   (commission)
  Machine: 9/9 repair steps, gold 60/60 aurei — ready to jump   (machine)
  ► The era's 20 years are over. Jump when you're ready (you can also stay).

> jump
You will leave AD 175 and arrive somewhere between AD 210 and AD 225: the machine's range is 35–50 years (repairs, 0 of 3 upgrades, and your time in this era). You can't come back.
What you leave behind (25,746 denarii and 34 aurei in hand):
  • Medicine debt 15.2 keeps growing 5% a year for 30 years after you leave (about 24.8 in a decade, 65.9 after 30 years) unless a crisis clears it. Paying it down now costs 1,143 denarii.
  • Governance debt 1.4 keeps growing 5% a year for 30 years after you leave (about 2.3 in a decade, 6.1 after 30 years) unless a crisis clears it. Paying it down now costs 105 denarii.
  • No institution you hold 10%+ of will look after Rome while you're away.
  • The machine will carry you 35–50 years (repairs, 0 of 3 upgrades, and your time in this era); exactly how far, you'll know when you arrive. Upgrades and more time here would lengthen it.
  • The 25,746 denarii in your hands stay behind and are lost unless you spend them, pay down debt or endow an institution.
  • Your gold: the machine can carry 10 aurei; you hold 34 aurei, so 24 aurei would be left behind and lost (deposit <n> or bury <n>, 1 Attention each; the bank takes up to 100 aurei, a jar holds 50 aurei).
  •   Denarii can't be carried, banked or buried usefully: change them into gold first (exchange <n> denarii).
Prepare: paydown <domain> <points> · endow <inst> <denarii|all> · audit <inst> · exchange <n> denarii · deposit <aurei> · bury <aurei>. Type 'jump' again to go, or anything else to stay.

> jump

The machine shudders. Decades pass in the dark... It carries you 45 years.

— Recognition —
You climb out of the machine in AD 220. The Tiber smells the same. Near the Porta Trigemina you find
the smith's workshop you funded by its soot-black wall. It is a stable now. Down the street, the
fountain you didn't choose was filled in long ago. An old woman calls the spot 'fever corner'.

— Wrongness —
Something is wrong, and it takes you a while to name it: beyond the few things you recognize, this is
Rome almost exactly as the history books describe AD 220. The coins are lighter and paler than you
remember, barely half silver now. Prices have climbed with every cut, and soldiers grumble that their
pay buys less each year.

— Personal echo —
Demetria's letters survive in a library copy. They name everyone who stayed to help the sick in 166.
You are not among them; she never thought to ask you.

— Discovery —
You ask at the guild halls and the Curia whether anyone remembers you. No one does: you never held
enough of anything in Rome to leave your mark on it. In your purse: the 10 aurei you carried, as good
as the day you left. The gold you couldn't carry, and didn't hide or bank (24 aurei), is long gone.

You can walk around Rome: visit market, changers, forges, curia or subura (no Attention).
Type 'learn more' for the Index and what became of your institutions, 'jump' to go on another 25–40 years, or 'quit'.

(after arrival) > learn more
Index (100 = matches real history; geometric mean of Medicine, Governance, Economy)
  Departure, AD 175: 101   Medicine 97  Governance 100  Economy 108
  Arrival,   AD 220: 101   Medicine 97  Governance 100  Economy 106
Institutions
  None you held 10% or more of.
Your gold: carried 10, deposited 0 (0 back), buried 0 (0 recovered), left behind 24; you hold 10 aurei now.
Crises while you were away
  AD 189: the pestilence returns to Rome under Commodus. At its worst two thousand die in a day; the emperor goes to Laurentum for the clean air. It is contained: about 36 thousand dead, 4% of Rome.


(after arrival) > why medicine
You have been away for 45 years. What happened in between is lost to you; the world simply is what it is now. (Try 'learn more'.)

(after arrival) > quit
